# FDF 1.0 — Plan 5: Contract and ship Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** fdf 1.0.0, ready to release. The validator checks spec 1.0 alone:
the pin is required, a pin it cannot validate is an F1 error that names the
fix and ends validation, and the 0.x code goes, with the 0.x fixtures
converted to 1.0 by `fdf migrate` or removed. `fdf migrate` gains what the
approved design asks beyond Plan 4 (`--skip`, the listing of a symbolic link
the move breaks, the refusal of a register's place taken by a file) and the
fixes Plan 4 left. One reader of a pin serves the validator and every
command, and each says why a bundle has none; `fdf init` writes over no old
bundle. The items the roadmap carried to Plan 5 are done. `fdf install`
writes 1.0's primer and skills, which, with the README, the spec indexes
and `CLAUDE.md`, teach 1.0, and the version is 1.0.0. Tagging and
releasing are left to the person running the plan.

**Architecture:**
- **`cli/internal/migrate`** adds `--skip` (`Options.Skip`, `skips` in
  `outside.go`), lists a broken symbolic link (`breaks`), refuses a file
  where a register goes (`registerFile`), reads code blocks through
  `links.Blocks` in `stripStatusTags`, asks an unpinned bundle of the stem
  layout, or one written for 1.0, for its pin (`stemTrail`, `laidOut1`),
  and ends its undo by removing the directories it made (`made`, `parents`,
  `git clean -fdx`). Its tests start from
  bundles in its own `testdata/`, since the conformance fixtures become
  1.0.
- **`cli/internal/bundle`** loses every 0.x path: `visitV0`, the version
  gates, `linkRe`, `sectionLinks`, `dirName`, `registerDirs` and the 0.6
  lexicon scan. `pinProblem` names the fix for any pin it does not
  validate, and validation stops there.
- **`cli/internal/fdfroot`**'s `Pin` and `PinOf` are the one reader of a
  pin, which the validator, `scaffold.Pin`, root resolution and migrate
  share; `Unclosed` names frontmatter that never closes, which the
  validator, the commands' gate and migrate each report; and `NoBundle`
  sends a root that holds Markdown but no `INDEX.md` to `fdf migrate`
  (`Unindexed`). **`cli/internal/scaffold`**'s gate says of a missing pin
  what the validator says, and `Init` refuses such a root.
- **`cli/internal/links`**' `Rendered` reads a reference where it is used,
  for F6's and F7's `sectionTargets`; **`cli/internal/layout`**'s
  `PlaceGroup` checks a group's place for `fdf mv`, and its messages name
  what a task and a task directory belong to.
- **`cli/internal/install`** writes the 1.0 primer and names `docs/fdf`,
  and recognizes an untouched 0.7 primer written for any root an install
  used (`primerV07`, `legacyRoot`, `recordedRoot`).
- **Documents**: the ten skills, `README.md`, `SPEC.md`, `spec/README.md`,
  `CLAUDE.md`, `embed.go`, the bug report template, the plugin manifest and
  the roadmap. Three tests hold what a version bump must keep in step: the
  spec indexes, the release version, and `layout`'s root against the
  current spec.

**Tech Stack:** Go 1.26, standard library only (`go.mod` has no
requirements), and git, which migrate runs and its tests drive. Task 6 runs
a Python 3 script, and Task 11 a `perl` one-liner, both given in full.

**Spec:** [2026-09-24-fdf-v1.0-design.md](../specs/2026-09-24-fdf-v1.0-design.md)
— §4 *Versioning*, §8 *Skills and docs*, *Rule changes* and *Testing*, and
the *Decisions* that Plan 4 settled and the design's owner approved on
2026-09-26 (13 to 21), with [`spec/1.0.md`](../../../spec/1.0.md) as the
normative format. The [roadmap](2026-09-24-fdf-v1.0-roadmap.md) places this
plan last of five; its *Carried forward from Plan 2*, *from Plan 3* and
*from Plan 4* sections list every item it takes on. Seven of Plan 4's
limits of `fdf migrate` stay as they are, by the owner's decision: the
README lists them for users, and the roadmap under *After 1.0*.

## Global Constraints

- Go 1.26, standard library only; add no module requirement.
- CI must pass: `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `gofmt -l .` printing
  nothing, `shellcheck install.sh`, and the lint that every
  `skills/*/SKILL.md` starts with `---` and has `name:` and `description:`
  lines. Run `gofmt -w` on every Go file you touch before committing.
- **`fdf validate`'s output changes only where a task means it to.** Tasks
  6 to 9 change the conformance fixtures and what the validator says of
  them, each as its steps give it; every other task leaves every fixture's
  output as it was.
- Work on **`claude/v1-plan-5-ship`**, the branch off `v1` that holds this
  plan, and merge it into `v1` by pull request. Merging `v1` into `main`,
  and tagging `v1.0.0` there, which starts the release, are left to the
  person running the plan.
- Commit messages end with the line
  `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`.
- Go source writes a byte order mark as the six-character escape `\uFEFF`,
  never as the character itself: `gofmt` rejects a literal one with
  "illegal byte order mark". Task 7's blocks hold one, in
  `cli/internal/bundle/collect.go`, and Task 8's hold several: in
  `cli/internal/fdfroot/root.go` and `root_test.go`, and in the text they
  replace in `cli/internal/bundle/validate.go`, `cli/internal/scaffold/pin.go`
  and `scaffold_test.go`. Copy the blocks byte for byte, never by
  retyping.
- Each change below is written as the exact text to replace and the text
  that replaces it, or as a file written whole, or as a script to run from
  the repository root. Copy each byte for byte; a block to replace occurs
  exactly once in its file at the point it is applied.
- Stage the paths each commit step names, never `git add -A` or
  `git add .`. If `go test` fails only on `TestConformanceFixtures/.DS_Store`,
  or `find testdata -name .DS_Store` prints anything, macOS Finder has left
  a file in `testdata/`: move it out of the repository, and never commit it.
- Tests that make git repositories need `git` on the path; they set
  `GIT_AUTHOR_*` and `GIT_COMMITTER_*` in their commands' environment.
  Tests that make symbolic links are skipped where the file system has
  none, and those that make a file read-only are skipped when run as root.

## Review Focus

Where this plan is most likely to go wrong, and the tests that hold each:

- **The conversion of the fixtures** (Task 6). 148 fixtures are migrated
  by a script and finished by rule; each one's `expect.txt` is what pins
  it. The script's options, the list of removed fixtures and each
  expectation are the review's matter; a fixture that fails for a reason
  other than its rule is the defect to look for.
  `TestConformanceFixtures`.
- **The validator's contract** (Task 7). A pin the validator does not
  check ends validation at F1, with the fix it needs; nothing of 0.x may
  survive in a path the 1.0 walk still takes, and the root's type check,
  which now sees only `SPEC.md`, must survive the merge of its maps.
  `TestPinProblemNamesTheFix`, the `pin-*`, `no-root-index` and
  `vendored-spec-typed-*` fixtures.
- **Reading a pin** (Task 8). A byte order mark, CRLF lines, quotes, a
  frontmatter line that does not parse, frontmatter that never closes, and a
  pin quoted in the body, where a documentation site might have one.
  `TestPinReadsTheFrontmatterLineByLine`,
  `TestUnclosedTellsFrontmatterThatNeverCloses`,
  `TestDefaultFindsABundleAtEitherLocation`, `TestMigratePinsTheFrontmatterAlone`,
  `TestMigrateRefusesFrontmatterThatNeverCloses` and the
  `index-frontmatter-*` fixtures.
- **What migrate now refuses, lists and removes** (Tasks 1 to 4): a glob
  read as git reads a pathspec; a symbolic link's target read from the
  project root as written, and with its directory and the root as the disk
  resolves them; an unpinned bundle written for 1.0, which must not be read
  as 0.x; `git clean -fdx` limited to the directories migrate made.
  `TestMigrateLeavesWhatSkipNamesAsItIs`,
  `TestMigrateListsASymbolicLinkTheMoveBreaks`,
  `TestMigrateReadsALinksTargetHoweverItIsSpelled`,
  `TestMigrateAsksABundleLaidOutFor10ForItsPin` and
  `TestMigrateSaysHowToBackOutAMigration`.
- **Upgrading an installed primer** (Task 11): a primer an earlier install
  wrote, for whichever root, is replaced; one a person edited never is.
  `TestUpgradeRefreshesShippedV07Primer` and
  `TestUpgradeRecognizesThePrimerOfTheRecordedRoot`.

## Before you start

```bash
git checkout claude/v1-plan-5-ship
go test ./... && gofmt -l .
```

Expected: every package prints `ok`, and `gofmt -l .` prints nothing. The
branch is `v1` plus this plan. Pushing it and opening the pull request into
`v1` are left to the person running the plan.

### Task 1: `fdf migrate --skip` leaves the files a glob names as they are

Design Decision 17. Migrate's outside pass reads every text file git tracks
outside the bundle, and rewrites each link into the bundle and each mention
of its path. Some files must keep their bytes whatever they say: applied SQL
migrations whose checksums a tool verifies, vendored code, recorded
fixtures. `--skip <glob>`, which may be given more than once, leaves the
files a glob names as they are:

- a glob reads from the project root as git reads a pathspec with `:(glob)`
  magic (`*` within a directory, `**` across them, a directory naming
  everything in it), through `git ls-files`;
- each link into the bundle and each mention of its path in a skipped file
  is listed with what is left behind, `in a skipped file`, in the order of
  the text, and the plan counts the files skipped on request;
- a skipped file is never written, so it is no part of the clean-tree check;
- a glob that names no file git tracks outside the bundle is refused, since
  a mistyped one would leave nothing as it is; so is `--skip` where migrate
  reads nothing outside the bundle: outside a git repository, for a bundle
  that is its own repository, and for one already at 1.0, which moves
  nothing.

**Files:**
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/migrate.go` (`Options.Skip`)
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/plan.go` (`newPlan`)
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/outside.go` (`skips`, `outside`)
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/report.go`
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/migrate_test.go`
- Modify: `cli/cmd/fdf/migrate_cmd.go`
- Modify: `cli/cmd/fdf/main_test.go`
- Modify: `cli/cmd/fdf/help.go` (the `migrate` topic)
- Modify: `spec/1.0.md` (*Upgrading from 0.x*)
- Modify: `CLAUDE.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `git(dir string, args ...string) (string, error)` in
  `relocate.go`; `plan.outside`.
- Produces: `Options.Skip []string`; `newPlan(root, pin, project, dest
  string, skip []string) (*plan, []string, error)`; `(p *plan) skips()
  ([]string, error)`; the reason `skippedFile`, "in a skipped file".

```go
}

// --skip leaves the files its globs name outside the bundle as they are, as
// applied SQL migrations must stay, and lists each link into the bundle and
// each mention of its path in them, in the order of the text. A glob reads
// from the project root as git reads one, a directory naming everything in
// it. A file migrate leaves as it is is no part of the clean-tree check.
func TestMigrateLeavesWhatSkipNamesAsItIs(t *testing.T) {
	project := gitProject(t, "valid-bugs-v07")
	files := map[string]string{
		"db/migrations/001_init.sql":    "-- The schema of docs/features/venues/opening-hours.md.\nCREATE TABLE hours (id int);\n",
		"db/migrations/v2/002_more.sql": "-- See docs/features.\n",
		"docs/old/notes.md":             "# Notes\n\nSee [hours](../features/venues/opening-hours.md), in docs/features/venues.\n",
		"README.md":                     "# Project\n\nThe bundle is in docs/features.\n",
	}
	for rel, content := range files {
		write(t, project, rel, content)
	}
	gitIn(t, project, "add", "-A")
	gitIn(t, project, "commit", "-qm", "references")
	files["db/migrations/001_init.sql"] += "-- not committed\n"
	write(t, project, "db/migrations/001_init.sql", files["db/migrations/001_init.sql"])
	var out bytes.Buffer
	if code := Run(Options{Root: filepath.Join(project, "docs", "features"), Project: project, Skip: []string{"db/migrations", "docs/old/*.md"}}, &out); code != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("migrate exit %d\n%s", code, out.String())
	}
	for rel, want := range files {
		if rel == "README.md" {
			want = "# Project\n\nThe bundle is in docs/fdf.\n"
		}
		if got := string(mustRead(t, filepath.Join(project, rel))); got != want {
			t.Errorf("%s:\n%s\nwant:\n%s", rel, got, want)
		}
	}
	for _, want := range []string{
		"  outside    1 mention in 1 file; 0 links in 0 files\n",
		"             left as they are: 4 mentions in a skipped file (listed below)\n",
		"             skipped on request: 3 files that --skip names\n",
		"\nleft as they are:\n" +
			"  db/migrations/001_init.sql:1  docs/features/venues/opening-hours.md  (in a skipped file)\n" +
			"  db/migrations/v2/002_more.sql:1  docs/features  (in a skipped file)\n" +
			"  docs/old/notes.md:3  ../features/venues/opening-hours.md  (in a skipped file)\n" +
			"  docs/old/notes.md:3  docs/features/venues  (in a skipped file)\n",
	} {
		if !strings.Contains(out.String(), want) {
			t.Errorf("the plan should say %q:\n%s", want, out.String())
		}
	}
}

// A --skip glob that names no file git tracks outside the bundle is refused,
// since a mistyped one would leave as they are none of the files it was
// meant to; and so is --skip where migrate reads nothing outside the bundle:
// outside a git repository, and for a bundle that is its own. Nothing is
// written.
func TestMigrateRefusesASkipThatNamesNothing(t *testing.T) {
	project := gitProject(t, "valid-bugs-v07")
	write(t, project, "db/migrations/001_init.sql", "-- docs/features\n")
	gitIn(t, project, "add", "-A")
	gitIn(t, project, "commit", "-qm", "sql")
	before := worktree(t, project)
	var out bytes.Buffer
	root := filepath.Join(project, "docs", "features")
	if code := Run(Options{Root: root, Project: project, Skip: []string{"db/migration/**", "db/migrations/**", "docs/features/**"}}, &out); code != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("migrate exit %d, want 1\n%s", code, out.String())
	}
	for _, want := range []string{
		"  --skip 'db/migration/**': names no file git tracks outside the bundle — a glob reads from the project root, " + project + "\n",
		"  --skip 'docs/features/**': names no file git tracks outside the bundle — a glob reads from the project root, " + project + "\n",
	} {
		if !strings.Contains(out.String(), want) {
			t.Errorf("migrate should refuse with %q:\n%s", want, out.String())
		}
	}
	if strings.Contains(out.String(), "'db/migrations/**'") {
		t.Errorf("a glob that names a file is no problem:\n%s", out.String())
	}
	if worktree(t, project) != before {
		t.Error("a refused migration writes nothing")
	}

	plain := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "docs", "features")
	copyFixtureTo(t, "valid-bugs-v07", plain)
	own := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "handbook")
	copyFixtureTo(t, "valid-bugs-v07", own)
	gitIn(t, own, "init", "-q")
	gitIn(t, own, "add", "-A")
	gitIn(t, own, "commit", "-qm", "bundle")
	for _, tc := range []struct{ root, project, want string }{
		{plain, "", "  --skip: the bundle is not in a git repository, so migrate reads no file outside it — run it without --skip\n"},
		{own, own, "  --skip: the bundle is its own git repository, so it has no outside — run it without --skip\n"},
	} {
		out.Reset()
		if code := Run(Options{Root: tc.root, Project: tc.project, Skip: []string{"db/**"}}, &out); code != 1 || !strings.Contains(out.String(), tc.want) {
			t.Errorf("migrate should refuse with %q: exit %d\n%s", tc.want, code, out.String())
		}
	}
	// A bundle at target moves nothing, and migrate reads nothing outside it.
	at := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "docs", "fdf")
	copyFixtureTo(t, "valid-v10", at)
	before = tree(t, at)
	out.Reset()
	if code := Run(Options{Root: at, Skip: []string{"db/**"}}, &out); code != 1 || out.String() != "cannot migrate: the bundle already pins fdf_version "+target+", and migrate reads no file outside a bundle at "+target+" — run it without --skip; the bundle was left as it is.\n" {
		t.Errorf("migrate should refuse --skip on a bundle at %s: exit %d\n%s", target, code, out.String())
	}
	if tree(t, at) != before {
		t.Error("a refused migration writes nothing")
	}
}

// --skip may be given more than once, and each glob reaches migrate: of two,
// the one that names no file is refused, and the other is not.
func TestMigrateTakesSkipMoreThanOnce(t *testing.T) {
	if _, err := exec.LookPath("git"); err != nil {
		t.Skip("git not available")
	}
	project := t.TempDir()
	for rel, content := range map[string]string{
		"docs/features/INDEX.md": "---\nfdf_version: \"0.7\"\n---\n\n# Bundle\n",
		"schema.sql":             "-- docs/features\n",
	} {
		if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(filepath.Join(project, rel)), 0o755); err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(project, rel), []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
	}
	for _, args := range [][]string{{"init", "-q"}, {"add", "-A"}, {"commit", "-qm", "bundle"}} {
		cmd := exec.Command("git", args...)
		cmd.Dir = project
		cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "GIT_AUTHOR_NAME=t", "GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL=t@t", "GIT_COMMITTER_NAME=t", "GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL=t@t")
		if out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("git %v: %v\n%s", args, err, out)
		}
	}
	code, out, _ := fdfRun("migrate", "--root", filepath.Join(project, "docs", "features"), "--skip", "schema.sql", "--skip", "nope/**", "--dry-run")
	if code != 1 || !strings.Contains(out, "  --skip 'nope/**': names no file git tracks outside the bundle") || strings.Contains(out, "--skip schema.sql") {
		t.Errorf("fdf migrate --skip schema.sql --skip 'nope/**': exit %d\n%s", code, out)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Skip what a glob names**

`Options.Skip` carries the globs to `newPlan`, which refuses them where
there is no outside, and otherwise reads them before the outside pass
(`skips`). The outside pass lists what a skipped file says of the bundle
instead of rewriting it, and the report counts the skipped files.

}
```

with:

```go
	To      string // where the bundle goes, absolute; "" for the default
	EnvRoot string // the bundle root FDF_ROOT_DIR names, absolute; "" when it is not set
	// Skip holds globs, read from the project root as git reads a pathspec
	// with :(glob) magic, naming files outside the bundle that the outside
	// pass leaves as they are, listing what they say of the bundle.
	Skip []string
}
```

10. In `cli/internal/migrate/outside.go`, replace:

```go
}

// outside plans the rewrite of every reference to the bundle from the rest
// of the project: in each file git tracks outside the bundle that holds
```

with:

```go
}

// skips reads which files --skip's globs name, each a pathspec from the
// project root read as git reads one with :(glob) magic: * within a
// directory, ** across them, and a directory for everything in it. Only a
// file git tracks outside the bundle is one the outside pass reads, and so
// skips; a glob that names none, most likely mistyped, would leave as they
// are none of the files it was meant to, and is refused.
func (p *plan) skips() ([]string, error) {
	var problems []string
	for _, g := range p.skip {
		out, err := git(p.project, "ls-files", "-z", "--", ":(glob)"+g)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		n := 0
		for _, f := range strings.Split(out, "\x00") {
			if f != "" && f != p.old && !strings.HasPrefix(f, p.old+"/") {
				p.skipped[f] = true
				n++
			}
		}
		if n == 0 {
			problems = append(problems, fmt.Sprintf("--skip %s: names no file git tracks outside the bundle — a glob reads from the project root, %s", quote(g), p.project))
		}
	}
	return problems, nil
}

// outside plans the rewrite of every reference to the bundle from the rest
// of the project: in each file git tracks outside the bundle that holds
```

  person to decide on (in a URL, after a longer path, in a link that leads elsewhere,
  or in a file a `--skip` glob names, which it leaves as it is), counting those in logs,
  and skipping `.gitmodules` and what `fdf install` manages, which it counts; a bundle
  that is its own repository has no outside. Refused before anything is written: a
  root inside a pinned bundle, or one that is a symbolic link, or whose `INDEX.md`,
  `LOG.md` or `SPEC.md` is one; a pin that is not a version, a newer one or an unknown
  0.x one; a v0.1 rename (`caseRenames`) or a 0.3 lift (`liftTrails`) onto a file that
  is there; a stray root Markdown file, a document named `index.md` or `log.md`, a
  practice, debt or bug beside a directory of Markdown, a group whose place in
  `features/` is taken or a `features` that is no directory, a directory of Markdown
  that is a symbolic link, a register migrate writes into that is one, whatever it
  holds; a destination that is not empty, inside the bundle, outside the project or
  inside `.git`, or behind a file; a tree that is not clean; a place git would ignore;
  and a `--skip` glob that names no file git tracks outside the bundle, or any where
  migrate reads nothing outside it: outside git, in a bundle that is its own
  repository, or at 1.0. A bundle at 1.0 moves nothing: its spec copy,
  indexes and Context stubs are restored, and one it would write through a symbolic
  link, the file's or its register's, is refused first. Validation runs with
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Format, vet and commit**

```bash
gofmt -l . && go vet ./... && go test ./...
git add cli/internal/migrate/migrate.go cli/internal/migrate/plan.go cli/internal/migrate/outside.go cli/internal/migrate/report.go cli/internal/migrate/migrate_test.go cli/cmd/fdf/migrate_cmd.go cli/cmd/fdf/main_test.go cli/cmd/fdf/help.go spec/1.0.md CLAUDE.md
git commit -m "feat(migrate): --skip leaves the files a glob names as they are

Design Decision 17: the outside pass reads every git-tracked text file,
and fdf migrate --skip <glob> leaves out the files a glob names, such as
applied SQL migrations whose checksums a tool verifies. A glob reads from
the project root as git reads a pathspec with :(glob) magic, and the flag
may be given more than once. Each link into the bundle and each mention
of its path in a skipped file is listed \"in a skipped file\", in the
order of the text, and the plan counts the files skipped; a skipped file
is never written, so it is no part of the clean-tree check.

A glob that names no file git tracks outside the bundle is refused, as a
mistyped one would leave nothing as it is, and so is --skip where migrate
reads nothing outside the bundle: outside a git repository, for a bundle
that is its own repository, and for one already at 1.0, which moves
nothing. The help, spec/1.0.md's Upgrading from 0.x and CLAUDE.md say
so.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
```

---

### Task 2: List the symbolic links a move breaks; refuse a file where a register goes

Design Decision 20, and a refusal Plan 4 left:

- **A symbolic link the move breaks, which migrate does not name again, is
  listed** with what is left behind. Migrate repoints a relative link in the
  bundle whose target a move changes (`relink`). A link that names its
  target by an absolute path cannot follow the move, whether it is in the
  bundle or outside it, and one outside the bundle is the project's, which
  migrate does not rewrite. Each such link whose target the move changes is
  listed as `a symbolic link the move breaks`, with no line number; one
  whose target stays where it is is not. `breaks` reads a target from the
  project root as written, and then with the target's directory and the
  root as the disk resolves them, since a link and the root migrate is
  given may each spell a path through a symbolic link (`/var` for
  `/private/var` on macOS). The summary counts them, and says what is left
  behind whether or not the bundle is in a git repository. A listed link's
  path is from the project root, which is the bundle's own when the bundle
  is its repository; outside a git repository, the bundle's own absolute
  links are listed too, with their paths from the file system's root.
- **A file where a register goes is refused.** A `practices` file at the
  bundle root stopped migrate partway, once the plan tried to write
  `practices/INDEX.md`. Every register migrate writes an index into is now
  checked before anything is written (`registerFile`), as a `features`
  file was, and so is each register the 1.0 repair path restores an index
  into.

**Files:**
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/plan.go` (`newPlan`, `refusals`,
  `registerFile`, `relink`, `breaks`)
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/outside.go` (`brokenLink`, `outside`)
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/report.go`
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/migrate.go` (`repair`)
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/migrate_test.go`
- Modify: `cli/cmd/fdf/help.go` (the `migrate` topic)
- Modify: `spec/1.0.md` (*Upgrading from 0.x*)
- Modify: `CLAUDE.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `links.Move.New`, `filepath.EvalSymlinks`.
- Produces: `registerFile(root, reg string) string`; `(p *plan)
  breaks(target string, mv links.Move) bool`; the reason `brokenLink`, "a
  symbolic link the move breaks". A listed item's `line` is 0 for a link.

// A symbolic link the move breaks, which migrate does not name again, is

func TestMigrateListsASymbolicLinkTheMoveBreaks(t *testing.T) {
	project := gitProject(t, "valid-bugs-v07")
	write(t, project, "assets/logo.png", "PNG")
	write(t, project, "docs/features/venues/diagram.png", "PNG")

		"assets/current.png":               "logo.png",

			t.Skip("symlinks unavailable:", err)
		}

		t.Fatalf("migrate exit %d\n%s", code, out.String())
	}
	for _, want := range []string{
		"             left as they are: 3 symbolic links the move breaks (listed below)\n",
		"  docs/fdf/features/venues/current.png  " + filepath.Join(project, "docs", "features", "venues", "diagram.png") + "  (a symbolic link the move breaks)\n",
		"  assets/diagram.png  ../docs/features/venues/diagram.png  (a symbolic link the move breaks)\n",
		"  assets/index  " + filepath.Join(project, "docs", "features", "INDEX.md") + "  (a symbolic link the move breaks)\n",
	} {
		if !strings.Contains(out.String(), want) {
			t.Errorf("the plan should say %q:\n%s", want, out.String())
		}
	}
	for _, not := range []string{"venues/logo.png ", "assets/current.png"} {
		if strings.Contains(out.String(), not) {
			t.Errorf("a link whose target stays is not listed (%s):\n%s", not, out.String())
		}
	}

	dir := t.TempDir()
	root := filepath.Join(dir, "docs", "features")
	copyFixtureTo(t, "valid-bugs-v07", root)
	write(t, root, "venues/diagram.png", "PNG")
	if err := os.Symlink(filepath.Join(root, "venues", "diagram.png"), filepath.Join(root, "venues", "current.png")); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	out.Reset()
	if code := Run(Options{Root: root}, &out); code != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("migrate exit %d\n%s", code, out.String())
	}
	for _, want := range []string{
		"             left as they are: 1 symbolic link the move breaks (listed below)\n",
		"  " + filepath.Join(dir, "docs", "fdf", "features", "venues", "current.png") + "  " + filepath.Join(root, "venues", "diagram.png") + "  (a symbolic link the move breaks)\n",
	} {

			t.Errorf("outside git, the plan should say %q:\n%s", want, out.String())
		}
	}

	// A bundle that is its own repository lists the link from its own root,
	// which is the project's.
	own := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "handbook")
	copyFixtureTo(t, "valid-bugs-v07", own)
	write(t, own, "venues/diagram.png", "PNG")
	if err := os.Symlink(filepath.Join(own, "venues", "diagram.png"), filepath.Join(own, "venues", "current.png")); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)

	gitIn(t, own, "init", "-q")
	gitIn(t, own, "add", "-A")
	gitIn(t, own, "commit", "-qm", "bundle")
	out.Reset()
	if code := Run(Options{Root: own, Project: own, DryRun: true}, &out); code != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("migrate exit %d\n%s", code, out.String())
	}
	if want := "  features/venues/current.png  " + filepath.Join(own, "venues", "diagram.png") + "  (a symbolic link the move breaks)\n"; !strings.Contains(out.String(), want) {
		t.Errorf("in a bundle that is its own repository, the plan should say %q:\n%s", want, out.String())
	}
}

// A symbolic link may spell its target through another link, as macOS
// spells /private/var as /var, and so may the root migrate is given: the
// target is read as written, and then with its directory and the project
// root as the disk resolves them, so the link is listed either way.
func TestMigrateReadsALinksTargetHoweverItIsSpelled(t *testing.T) {
	project := gitProject(t, "valid-bugs-v07")
	alias := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "alias")
	if err := os.Symlink(project, alias); err != nil {
		t.Skip("symlinks unavailable:", err)
	}
	write(t, project, "docs/features/venues/diagram.png", "PNG")
	targets := [][2]string{
		{"via-project.png", filepath.Join(project, "docs", "features", "venues", "diagram.png")},
		{"via-alias.png", filepath.Join(alias, "docs", "features", "venues", "diagram.png")},
	}
	for _, l := range targets {
		if err := os.Symlink(l[1], filepath.Join(project, "docs", "features", "venues", l[0])); err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
	}
	gitIn(t, project, "add", "-A")
	gitIn(t, project, "commit", "-qm", "links")
	for _, from := range []string{project, alias} {
		var out bytes.Buffer
		if code := Run(Options{Root: filepath.Join(from, "docs", "features"), Project: from, DryRun: true}, &out); code != 0 {
			t.Fatalf("migrate from %s: exit %d\n%s", from, code, out.String())
		}
		for _, l := range targets {
			if want := "  docs/fdf/features/venues/" + l[0] + "  " + l[1] + "  (a symbolic link the move breaks)\n"; !strings.Contains(out.String(), want) {
				t.Errorf("migrated from %s, the plan should say %q:\n%s", from, want, out.String())
			}
		}
	}
}

// A dry run changes nothing, git's own files included: git status, which
// would refresh the index, takes no lock.

// registerFile says why the place of the register reg, at the bundle root
// at root, is taken, or "" when it is not: a file is there, where migrate
// would write the register's index. One that is a symbolic link is
// linkedRegister's to name.
func registerFile(root, reg string) string {
	if fi, err := os.Lstat(filepath.Join(root, reg)); err == nil && !fi.IsDir() && fi.Mode()&os.ModeSymlink == 0 {
		return fmt.Sprintf("%s: not a directory, where the %s/ register goes — move it out of the bundle root", reg, reg)
	}
	return ""
}

// breaks reports whether the move changes where the file at the absolute
// path target is, so that a symbolic link naming it no longer finds it. The
// path is read from base as written, then as the disk resolves base, the
// target's directory, or both, since a link and the root migrate is given
// may each spell a path through a symbolic link (/var and /private/var on
// macOS). The target's directory is there before the move, even when the
// target is not.
func (p *plan) breaks(target string, mv links.Move) bool {
	target = filepath.Clean(target)
	bases, targets := []string{p.base}, []string{target}
	if real, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(p.base); err == nil && real != p.base {
		bases = append(bases, real)
	}
	if dir, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(filepath.Dir(target)); err == nil {
		if real := filepath.Join(dir, filepath.Base(target)); real != target {
			targets = append(targets, real)
		}
	}
	for _, t := range targets {
		for _, b := range bases {
			if r, err := filepath.Rel(b, t); err == nil && filepath.IsLocal(r) {
				rel := filepath.ToSlash(r)
				n, moved := mv.New(rel)
				return moved && n != rel
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

// isLog reports whether rel is a log, whose words record what things were
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Format, vet and commit**

```bash
gofmt -l . && go vet ./... && go test ./...
git add cli/internal/migrate/plan.go cli/internal/migrate/outside.go cli/internal/migrate/report.go cli/internal/migrate/migrate.go cli/internal/migrate/migrate_test.go cli/cmd/fdf/help.go spec/1.0.md CLAUDE.md
git commit -m "feat(migrate): list the symbolic links a move breaks; refuse a register a file takes

Design Decision 20: a symbolic link that names its target by an absolute
path cannot follow the move, and one migrate breaks is now listed with
what is left behind, in the bundle or outside it (breaks, which reads the
target from the project root as written, and then with the target's
directory and the root as the disk resolves them, since either may be
spelled through a symbolic link). So is a git-tracked symbolic link
outside the bundle whose relative target the move changes: migrate names
again only the bundle's own, and nothing it breaks goes unmentioned. A
link whose target stays where it is is not listed. A listed link has no
line number, and the plan shows its path as a person reads it: from the
project root, which is the bundle's own when the bundle is its
repository, and outside a git repository from the file system's root.
The summary counts them as symbolic links the move breaks, and says what
is left behind whether or not the bundle is in a git repository.

A file at the bundle root where a register goes, such as a practices
file, stopped migrate partway, since the register's index could not be
written; it is now refused before anything is written, as a features
file was (registerFile), and so is one where a 1.0 bundle's repair would
restore an index.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
```

---

### Task 3: Read status tags' code as `links` does; ask an unpinned bundle of a later layout for its pin

Two defects Plan 4 left in migrate, and a test gap:

- **`stripStatusTags` kept a fence test of its own.** It removes the status
  tags older tools wrote after index listings (` (**draft**)`), and leaves
  those in code alone. Its own test took a prose line that starts with a run
  of backticks and closes it on the same line, a code span, for an opening
  fence: the listings after it kept their tags, and a sample's lost its own.
  It now reads code blocks as the link engine does (`links.Blocks`).
- **An unpinned bundle in the stem layout was told to rename every trail
  file.** A bundle that pins nothing is read as 0.1 to 0.3, and their
  pre-flight read `slug.spec.md` as a dotted name that 0.4 reserves. Such a
  bundle was written for 0.4 or later and has lost its pin: when a trail
  file sits beside its document (`stemTrail`), migrate asks for the pin
  instead, one of 0.4 to 0.7, naming one trail file, and writes nothing.
  A bundle written for 1.0 that has lost its pin would be read the same way,
  and its registers moved into `features/`: when it holds
  `features/INDEX.md`, spelled so, or a `SPEC.md` that is 1.0's
  (`laidOut1`), migrate says so and writes nothing, since it needs only its
  pin; for a bundle that was older all along, it adds that fdf 0.7 read a
  bundle with no pin as 0.2.
- **The back-out test** covered a plain directory and a submodule; it now
  also backs out a bundle that is its own repository.

**Files:**
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/plan.go` (`stripStatusTags`, `newPlan`)
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/migrate.go` (`Run`, `stemTrail`, `laidOut1`)
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/migrate_test.go`
- Modify: `CLAUDE.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `links.Blocks(text string) []links.Span`.
- Produces: `stemTrail(root string) string`, the first trail file it finds
  beside its document, or ""; `laidOut1(root string) string`, what shows
  that an unpinned bundle was written for 1.0, or "".

`TestMigrateSaysHowToBackOutAMigration` gains a bundle that is its own

}

// A bundle that pins nothing is read as 0.1 to 0.3, whose layout has no
// trail files. One in the stem layout was written for 0.4 or later and has
// lost its pin: migrate asks for the pin, rather than for each trail file
// to be renamed, and writes nothing.
func TestMigrateAsksAnUnpinnedStemBundleForItsPin(t *testing.T) {
	root := copyFixture(t, "valid-bugs-v07")
	write(t, root, "INDEX.md", strings.Replace(string(mustRead(t, filepath.Join(root, "INDEX.md"))), "---\nfdf_version: \"0.7\"\n---\n\n", "", 1))
	before := tree(t, root)
	var out bytes.Buffer
	if code := Run(Options{Root: root}, &out); code != 1 || out.String() != "cannot migrate — fix these first (bundle left unchanged):\n"+
		"  INDEX.md: pins no fdf_version, so migrate reads the bundle as 0.1 to 0.3, whose layout has no trail file such as bugs/hours-off-by-one.log.md — pin the version it was written for, one of 0.4 to 0.7, in INDEX.md, and run migrate again\n" {
		t.Errorf("migrate asks for the pin: exit %d\n%s", code, out.String())
	}
	if tree(t, root) != before {
		t.Error("a refused migration changes nothing")
	}
}

// A bundle written for 1.0 that has lost its pin is no bundle from before
// 1.0: read as 0.1 to 0.3, its registers would move into features/. Migrate
// says what shows it, features/INDEX.md or a SPEC.md that is 1.0's, asks for
// the pin, and writes nothing.
func TestMigrateAsksABundleLaidOutFor10ForItsPin(t *testing.T) {
	root := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "fdf")
	var made bytes.Buffer
	if code := scaffold.Init(root, &made); code != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("init exit %d\n%s", code, made.String())
	}
	write(t, root, "INDEX.md", strings.Replace(string(mustRead(t, filepath.Join(root, "INDEX.md"))), "fdf_version: \""+target+"\"\n", "", 1))
	for i, sign := range []string{"it holds features/INDEX.md", "its SPEC.md is spec " + target + "'s"} {
		if i > 0 {
			// Without features/INDEX.md, the copy of the spec shows it.
			if err := os.Remove(filepath.Join(root, "features", "INDEX.md")); err != nil {
				t.Fatal(err)
			}
		}
		before := tree(t, root)
		var out bytes.Buffer
		want := "cannot migrate: INDEX.md pins no fdf_version, but " + sign + ", as in a bundle written for spec " + target + " — if it was, pin fdf_version: \"" + target + "\" in INDEX.md, and there is nothing to migrate; if it was written for an older version, pin that one (fdf 0.7 read a bundle with no pin as 0.2), and run migrate again; the bundle was left as it is.\n"
		if code := Run(Options{Root: root, DryRun: true}, &out); code != 1 || out.String() != want {
			t.Errorf("migrate asks for the pin: exit %d\n%s", code, out.String())
		}
		if tree(t, root) != before {
			t.Error("a refused migration changes nothing")
		}
	}
	// A group of an older bundle may be named features, and v0.1 spelled its
	// index index.md: that is no sign, though a disk that ignores case opens
	// it as features/INDEX.md.
	old := t.TempDir()
	write(t, old, "INDEX.md", "# Bundle\n")
	write(t, old, "features/index.md", "# Features\n")
	var out bytes.Buffer
	Run(Options{Root: old, DryRun: true}, &out)
	if strings.Contains(out.String(), "as in a bundle written for spec") {
		t.Errorf("a group's index.md is no sign of %s:\n%s", target, out.String())
	}
}

// A migrate that finds the pin already current looks identical to a migrate
// that is simply too old to know about newer versions — which is what happens

// repository that holds them, and the commands do put everything back: for
// a plain directory and for a submodule, with a file outside the bundle
// rewritten.
func TestMigrateSaysHowToBackOutAMigration(t *testing.T) {
	const says = "      to back the migration out instead, run these commands; the first takes back the\n" +
		"      marks of `git add -N`, on which git stash and git clean would trip:\n"

```go
// repository that holds them, and the commands do put everything back: for
// a plain directory and for a submodule, with a file outside the bundle
// rewritten, and for a bundle that is its own repository.
func TestMigrateSaysHowToBackOutAMigration(t *testing.T) {
	const says = "      to back the migration out instead, run these commands; the first takes back the\n" +
		"      marks of `git add -N`, on which git stash and git clean would trip:\n"
	for _, kind := range []string{"a plain directory", "a submodule", "its own repository"} {
		var project, root string
		switch kind {
		case "a submodule":
			project = gitSuperproject(t, "valid-bugs-v07")
		case "its own repository":
			project = filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "handbook")
			copyFixtureTo(t, "valid-bugs-v07", project)
			gitIn(t, project, "init", "-q")
			gitIn(t, project, "add", "-A")
			gitIn(t, project, "commit", "-qm", "bundle")
			root = project
		default:
			project = gitProject(t, "valid-bugs-v07")
		}
		if root == "" {
			root = filepath.Join(project, "docs", "features")
			write(t, project, "README.md", "# Project\n\nThe bundle is in docs/features.\n")
			gitIn(t, project, "commit", "-qam", "readme")
		}
		before := worktree(t, project)
		var out bytes.Buffer
		if code := Run(Options{Root: root, Project: project}, &out); code != 0 {
			t.Fatalf("%s: migrate exit %d\n%s", kind, code, out.String())
		}
		if root != project {
			if readme := string(mustRead(t, filepath.Join(project, "README.md"))); !strings.Contains(readme, "docs/fdf") {
				t.Fatalf("%s: the test needs a file outside the bundle rewritten:\n%s", kind, readme)
			}
		}
		_, rest, ok := strings.Cut(out.String(), says)
		if !ok {
			t.Fatalf("%s: the next steps say how to back the migration out:\n%s", kind, out.String())
		}
		var cmds []string

2. In `cli/internal/migrate/plan.go`, replace:

```go
// stripStatusTags removes the status tag older tools put after an index
// listing (` (**draft**)`), outside code, and says how many it removed.
// Nothing kept it current, so it went stale as soon as the document moved on;
// a status lives only in its document.
func stripStatusTags(text string) (string, int) {
	lines := strings.Split(text, "\n")
	fence, removed := "", 0
	for i, line := range lines {
		t := strings.TrimSpace(line)
		if fence != "" {
			if strings.HasPrefix(t, fence) {
				fence = ""
			}
			continue
		}
		if strings.HasPrefix(t, "```") || strings.HasPrefix(t, "~~~") {
			fence = t[:3]
			continue
		}
		if m := statusTagRe.FindStringSubmatch(line); m != nil {
			lines[i] = m[1] + m[2]
			removed++
		}
	}
	return strings.Join(lines, "\n"), removed
}
```

with:

```go
// stripStatusTags removes the status tag older tools put after an index
// listing (` (**draft**)`), outside code blocks, which it reads as the link
// engine does (links.Blocks), and says how many it removed. Nothing kept it
// current, so it went stale as soon as the document moved on; a status
// lives only in its document.
func stripStatusTags(text string) (string, int) {
	blocks := links.Blocks(text)
	inBlock := func(at int) bool {
		for _, b := range blocks {
			if b.Start <= at && at < b.End {
				return true
			}
		}
		return false
	}
	var b strings.Builder
	removed, pos := 0, 0
	for _, line := range strings.SplitAfter(text, "\n") {
		at := pos
		pos += len(line)
		body, nl := strings.CutSuffix(line, "\n")
		if m := statusTagRe.FindStringSubmatch(body); m != nil && !inBlock(at) {
			line = m[1] + m[2]
			if nl {
				line += "\n"
			}
			removed++
		}
		b.WriteString(line)
	}
	return b.String(), removed
}
```

with:

```go
}

// laidOut1 names what shows that the bundle at root, which pins nothing, was
// written for spec 1.0: features/INDEX.md, the index of the register 1.0
// files every feature in, or a SPEC.md that is 1.0's; or "" when neither is
// there. A bundle from before 1.0 held a features/INDEX.md only in a group it
// named features, which its pin, once written in, tells apart.
func laidOut1(root string) string {
	index := filepath.Join(root, "features", "INDEX.md")
	switch {
	case exists(index) && onDisk(index) == index:
		return "it holds features/INDEX.md"
	case specCurrent(root):
		return "its SPEC.md is spec " + target + "'s"
	}
	return ""
}

5. In `cli/internal/migrate/migrate.go`, replace:

```go
}

// featureIsDraft reports whether the sibling feature document of a paired
// directory carries status: draft.
```

with:

```go
}

// stemTrailRe is a trail file of the stem layout, 0.4's and later's: a
// <slug>.<role>.md beside its document.
var stemTrailRe = regexp.MustCompile(`^([a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*)\.(spec|plan|test|surface|log)\.md$`)

// stemTrail is the first trail file of the stem layout in a group of the
// bundle at root, beside the document it belongs to, or "" when there is
// none: the mark of a bundle written for 0.4 or later.
func stemTrail(root string) string {
	groups, _ := os.ReadDir(root)
	for _, g := range groups {
		if !g.IsDir() || strings.HasPrefix(g.Name(), ".") {
			continue
		}
		files, _ := os.ReadDir(filepath.Join(root, g.Name()))
		for _, f := range files {
			if m := stemTrailRe.FindStringSubmatch(f.Name()); m != nil && exists(filepath.Join(root, g.Name(), m[1]+".md")) {
				return g.Name() + "/" + f.Name()
			}
		}
	}
	return ""
}

// featureIsDraft reports whether the sibling feature document of a paired
// directory carries status: draft.
```

---

### Task 4: An undo removes the directories migrate made

The undo migrate prints when it stops partway, and the back-out it prints
once it is done, put every file back, but left behind what a person's tools
had put in the directories migrate made. A Finder `.DS_Store`, which git
ignores, stayed at a group's place in `features/`, and the next run refused
that place as taken. The directories `--to` made above the destination
stayed too, empty.

Migrate now records the directories it makes that were not there
(`findMade`, at the end of `newPlan`): `features/`, or each group's place
in it when `features/` is there already, each register it writes an index
into, and each directory above the destination that did not exist. The
undo ends by removing them whole with `git clean -fdx -- <paths>`, which
takes the files git ignores too: nothing was there before migrate made
them. For a submodule, the bundle's own directories are cleaned in the
submodule and those above the destination in the superproject.

**Files:**
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/plan.go` (`plan.made`, `plan.parents`,
  `findMade`)
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/relocate.go` (`undo`)
- Modify: `cli/internal/migrate/migrate_test.go`
- Modify: `CLAUDE.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `relSlash`, `q` and `quoteAll` in `relocate.go`.
- Produces: `(p *plan) findMade(dest string)`, which fills `p.made`, paths
  from the bundle's root, and `p.parents`, the absolute paths of the
  directories above the destination that the move creates.

2. In `cli/internal/migrate/plan.go`, replace:

```go
		return nil, problems, err

	p.findMade(dest)

}

// findMade finds the directories migrate makes that are not there now,
// which an undo removes whole once git has put the rest back, with any
// hidden file a person's tools have left in them since, such as a Finder
// .DS_Store: one left in a group's place in features/ would stop the next
// run, which finds that place taken. In the bundle they are features/, or
// each group's place in it when features/ is there already, and each
// register migrate writes an index into; above the destination, the
// directories relocate makes to hold it.
func (p *plan) findMade(dest string) {
	if _, err := os.Lstat(filepath.Join(p.root, "features")); err != nil {
		p.made = append(p.made, "features")
	} else {
		for _, g := range p.groups {
			p.made = append(p.made, "features/"+g)
		}
	}
	for _, reg := range layout.Registers {
		if _, err := os.Lstat(filepath.Join(p.root, reg)); err != nil && reg != "features" && p.exists(reg+"/INDEX.md") {
			p.made = append(p.made, reg)
		}
	}
	if !p.relocates() {
		return
	}
	for d := filepath.Dir(dest); filepath.Dir(d) != d; d = filepath.Dir(d) {
		if _, err := os.Lstat(d); err == nil {
			break
		}
		p.parents = append(p.parents, d)
	}
}
```

// with mv, which takes back the files git ignores too, and a submodule with
// git mv, then .gitmodules as it was. Then git restores what migrate
// changed, and removes what it wrote, and last the directories migrate made
// go whole, the files git ignores in them too (git clean -x): nothing was
// there before migrate made them.
func (p *plan) undo(project string) []string {
	q := quote
	// clean removes, in the repository at dir, the directories migrate
	// made in the bundle, at bundle from dir, and the paths in more.
	clean := func(dir, bundle string, more ...string) []string {
		var paths []string
		for _, d := range p.made {
			paths = append(paths, path.Join(bundle, d))
		}
		if paths = append(paths, more...); len(paths) == 0 {
			return nil
		}
		return []string{fmt.Sprintf("git -C %s clean -fdx -- %s", q(dir), quoteAll(paths))}
	}
	var parents []string
	for _, d := range p.parents {
		parents = append(parents, relSlash(project, d))
	}
	switch {
	case project == "":
		return []string{"the bundle is not in a git repository: restore it from a copy"}
	case project == p.root:
		return append([]string{fmt.Sprintf("git -C %s checkout -- . && git -C %s clean -fd", q(project), q(project))}, clean(project, "")...)
	}
	var lines []string
	if p.submodule {
		abs := filepath.Join(project, filepath.FromSlash(p.old))
		old := q(abs)
		if p.relocated {
			lines = append(lines, fmt.Sprintf("git -C %s mv %s %s", q(project), q(p.new), q(p.old)),

### Task 6: Convert the conformance fixtures to spec 1.0

Once the validator reads 1.0 alone (Task 7), a fixture that pins 0.x tests
nothing but F1. Of the 201 fixtures, design *Testing* keeps every one whose
rule 1.0 keeps, converted by running the new `fdf migrate` over it, as a
bundle nobody has touched since would be, and removes the rest:

- **Converted, 148 fixtures.** Each is migrated with the `fdf` this branch
  builds, then tidied so that it fails only for the rule it is about: the
  migration's own entry leaves `LOG.md` (the file goes if migrate made it);
  the vendored `SPEC.md` becomes the short stand-in the 1.0 fixtures keep; a
  Context document migrate had to create is filled rather than left a stub;
  and a test document from before 0.7 has its `# Test Cases` bullets that
  name a scenario turned into the `## <scenario name>` headings 0.7
  matches. Each takes its name without its `-vNN` suffix, and its
  `expect.txt` names the paths and IDs migrate gave it.
- **Four hand edits**, which the script makes: the ID of a feature that
  does not exist, which migrate cannot know to be a feature's, is written in
  full in three fixtures, and `bug-outside-bugs` loses the root file that the
  closed root now reports on its own. `context-missing-surfaces-v04`
  becomes `context-missing-with-feature`, and still lacks `SURFACES.md`,
  the document it is about: the script drops the stub migrate makes for
  it.
- **Renamed, 15 fixtures**: the 1.0 fixtures lose their `-v10` suffix, and
  `valid-v10` is `valid-every-shape`.
- **Removed, 35 fixtures**: those of a document at the root — a feature,
  change, practice, debt or log, or a Context document by another name —
  which the closed root now reports whatever its type (`closed-root`; F3
  still checks the type of `SPEC.md`, the one other document there, which
  Task 7's `vendored-spec-typed-*` fixtures hold); those of rules 1.0 no
  longer has (0.2's paired directories and nested trail names, 0.5's
  one-line declaration entries and a removal and addition of one name,
  0.6's lexicon scan of the Gherkin alone and of declared names alone, an
  adopted feature on a 0.6 pin, and a timestamp 0.6 did not check); and
  those another fixture repeats: the 0.2 and 0.3 twins of the 0.4 fixtures,
  `valid-bugs-v07` beside `valid-bugs-v10`, one missing Context document of
  three, and a debt's R1 beside `resource-missing-v10`.
- **Kept as they are, 3 fixtures**, for Task 7: `missing-root-index`,
  `pin-newer-than-fdf` and `wrong-fdf-version-pin`.

The tests that copied a converted fixture follow it: `fdf log`, `fdf mv`
and `fdf lexicon` refuse `valid-bugs` pinned back to 0.7 (`pinTo`), the own
repository test validates `valid-adopted` at `repo/docs/fdf`, and the
submodule test mounts `valid-done` at `docs/fdf`.

**Files:**
- Modify: `testdata/` (148 fixtures converted, 15 renamed, 35 removed)
- Modify: `cli/internal/logs/logs_test.go`, `cli/internal/refactor/mv_test.go`
  (`pinTo`, and the fixtures' new names)
- Modify: `cli/internal/refactor/lexicon_test.go`,
  `cli/cmd/fdf/main_test.go`, `cli/internal/bundle/submodule_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `fdf migrate`, built from this branch.
- Produces: `pinTo(t, root, pin)` in the `logs` and `refactor` tests, which
  rewrites a bundle's pin.

- [ ] **Step 1: Point the tests at the converted fixtures**

1. In `cli/internal/logs/logs_test.go`, replace:

```go
}

func logEntry(t *testing.T, root, id, entry string) string {
	t.Helper()
```

with:

```go
}

// pinTo makes the bundle at root one that pins version, as a bundle nobody
// has migrated does.
func pinTo(t *testing.T, root, version string) {
	t.Helper()
	index := read(t, root, "INDEX.md")
	pinned := strings.Replace(index, `fdf_version: "1.0"`, `fdf_version: "`+version+`"`, 1)
	if pinned == index {
		t.Fatalf("INDEX.md pins no 1.0 to change:\n%s", index)
	}
	if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(root, "INDEX.md"), []byte(pinned), 0o644); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}

// pinTo makes the bundle at root one that pins version, as a bundle nobody
// has migrated does.
func pinTo(t *testing.T, root, version string) {
	t.Helper()
	index := read(t, root, "INDEX.md")
	pinned := strings.Replace(index, `fdf_version: "1.0"`, `fdf_version: "`+version+`"`, 1)
	if pinned == index {
		t.Fatalf("INDEX.md pins no 1.0 to change:\n%s", index)
	}
	write(t, root, "INDEX.md", pinned)
}
```

	task := filepath.Join(bundleRepo, "features", "wdise", "example", "01-do-thing.md")
	raw, _ := os.ReadFile(task)
	patched := strings.Replace(string(raw), "status: done\n", "status: done\nresource: services/thing\n", 1)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Convert the fixtures**

Run from the repository root. The script is written to a temporary
directory with the `fdf` it drives, and removed with it; it stages its
removals and renames with `git rm` and `git mv`:

~~~bash
set -e
d=$(mktemp -d)
go build -o "$d/fdf" ./cli/cmd/fdf
cat > "$d/convert.py" <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""convert.py REPO FDF: convert the conformance fixtures to spec 1.0.

A fixture of a rule 1.0 keeps is migrated with fdf migrate, as a bundle
nobody has touched since would be, then tidied so that it fails only for
the rule it is about: the migration's log entry goes, the vendored SPEC.md
becomes the short stand-in the 1.0 fixtures keep, a Context document
migrate had to create is filled rather than left a stub, and a 0.x test
document's cases become the `## <scenario name>` headings 0.7 matches. It
takes the name it had without its -vNN suffix. A fixture of a rule 1.0 no
longer has, or one that repeats another across versions, is removed. The
1.0 fixtures lose their -v10 suffix.
"""
import os, re, shutil, subprocess, sys, tempfile

REPO, FDF = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
TD = os.path.join(REPO, "testdata")

# Fixtures of rules 1.0 no longer has, and those another repeats.
REMOVE = """
adopted-on-v06-pin change-at-bundle-root-v05 change-remove-and-add-same-name-v05
context-docs-valid context-missing-with-feature context-stub-blocks-feature
context-wrong-name-v04 debt-at-bundle-root-v06 debt-resource-missing-v06
declaration-line-after-entry-v05 depends-on-cycle directory-without-feature
domain-banned-word-in-gherkin-v06 domain-missing-v06 domain-prose-ignored-v06
domain-strict-declaration-names-only-v06 done-with-open-task
draft-with-paired-directory f8-bullet-cases-v06 lowercase-trail-spec
plan-not-linking-all-tasks planned-without-testmd practice-at-bundle-root-v06
root-log-type-v03 root-log-type-v04 spec-reference-at-root specified-without-spec
stray-file-in-paired-directory structural-type-at-bundle-root testmd-missing-scenario
timestamp-unchecked-v06 uppercase-nonreserved valid-bugs-v07 valid-done valid-minimal
""".split()

# Fixtures converted with options: a name of their own; drop the stubs
# migrate creates for these Context documents (the fixture is about one
# missing); remove these files before migrating (what the closed root now
# reports on its own); or edit a file once migrated: an ID of a feature that
# does not exist, which migrate cannot know to be one, is written in full.
OPTIONS = {
    "context-missing-surfaces-v04": {"name": "context-missing-with-feature", "drop_stubs": ["SURFACES.md"]},
    "valid-v10": {"name": "valid-every-shape"},
    "bug-outside-bugs-v07": {"pre_remove": ["misfiled-root.md"]},
    "bug-affects-unknown-feature-v07": {"edit": [("bugs/ghost.md", "affects: venues/ghost", "affects: features/venues/ghost")]},
    "replaced-by-unknown-v07": {"edit": [("features/venues/opening-hours.md", "replaced-by: venues/nowhere", "replaced-by: features/venues/nowhere")]},
    "replaced-by-list-unknown-v07": {"edit": [("features/venues/opening-hours.md", "replaced-by: [venues/nowhere]", "replaced-by: [features/venues/nowhere]")]},
}

# Fixtures that keep their pin until the validator's contract changes.
LATER = {"missing-root-index", "pin-newer-than-fdf", "wrong-fdf-version-pin"}

SPEC_STANDIN = "---\ntype: Reference\ntitle: Feature Document Format spec\ndescription: The FDF v1.0 specification this bundle conforms to.\ntimestamp: 2026-09-24\n---\n\nThe vendored specification.\n"

def context(title, body):
    return f"---\ntype: Context\ntitle: {title}\ndescription: Filled.\ntimestamp: 2026-09-23T00:00:00Z\n---\n\n{body}"

FILLED = {
    "STACK.md": context("Stack", "# Stack\n\nGo 1.26.\n"),
    "ARCHITECTURE.md": context("Architecture", "# Architecture\n\nA modular monolith.\n"),
    "SURFACES.md": context("Surfaces", "# Surfaces\n\nAn HTTP API.\n"),
    "INFRA.md": context("Infra", "# Infra\n\nBuilt and tested in CI.\n"),
    "DOMAIN.md": context("Domain Language", "# Terms\n\n## Example\nThe thing this bundle is about.\n"),
}

CASE_RE = re.compile(r"^[-*] (?:Scenario: )?(?P<name>.+?) — (?P<ver>.+)$")
SCENARIO_RE = re.compile(r"(?m)^\s*Scenario(?: Outline)?:\s*(\S[^\n]*)")
MIGRATED_RE = re.compile(r"## \d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}\r?\n\* \*\*Migrated\*\*: [^\r\n]*\r?\n(?:\r?\n)?")


def git(*args):
    subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, *args], check=True, capture_output=True)


def pin_of(root):
    try:
        m = re.search(r'fdf_version:\s*["\']?([^"\'\s]+)', open(os.path.join(root, "INDEX.md")).read())
        return m.group(1) if m else ""
    except FileNotFoundError:
        return ""


def headings(text, scenarios):
    """The `# Test Cases` bullets of a 0.x test document that name a scenario, as headings."""
    if "\r" in text:
        return text
    out, in_cases, changed = [], False, False
    for line in text.split("\n"):
        if line.startswith("# "):
            in_cases = line.strip() == "# Test Cases"
        m = CASE_RE.match(line) if in_cases else None
        if m and m.group("name").strip() in scenarios:
            out += ["", "## " + m.group("name").strip(), "", m.group("ver").strip(), ""]
            changed = True
        else:
            out.append(line)
    if not changed:
        return text
    return re.sub(r"\n{3,}", "\n\n", "\n".join(out)).rstrip("\n") + "\n"


def convert(old, new, opts, work):
    src = os.path.join(TD, old)
    dst = os.path.join(work, old)
    shutil.copytree(src, dst, symlinks=True)
    rel = "bundle" if os.path.isdir(os.path.join(dst, "bundle")) else "repo/docs/features"
    root = os.path.join(dst, rel)
    pin = pin_of(root)
    for f in opts.get("pre_remove", []):
        os.remove(os.path.join(root, f))
    before = {f for f in os.listdir(root)}
    had_log = "LOG.md" in before
    r = subprocess.run([FDF, "migrate", "--root", root], capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=work)
    if "done: migrated" not in r.stdout:
        sys.exit(f"{old}: migrate did not finish:\n{r.stdout}")
    if rel.startswith("repo"):
        root = os.path.join(dst, "repo/docs/fdf")
    # The migration's own log entry is no part of the fixture.
    log = os.path.join(root, "LOG.md")
    text = MIGRATED_RE.sub("", open(log, newline="").read(), count=1)
    if had_log:
        eol = "\r\n" if "\r\n" in text else "\n"
        open(log, "w", newline="").write(text.rstrip("\r\n") + eol)
    else:
        os.remove(log)
    open(os.path.join(root, "SPEC.md"), "w").write(SPEC_STANDIN)
    for rel_path, a, b in opts.get("edit", []):
        p = os.path.join(root, rel_path)
        text = open(p, newline="").read()
        assert text.count(a) == 1, (old, rel_path, a)
        open(p, "w", newline="").write(text.replace(a, b))
    for name, filled in FILLED.items():
        if name in before:
            continue
        if name in opts.get("drop_stubs", []):
            os.remove(os.path.join(root, name))
        else:
            open(os.path.join(root, name), "w").write(filled)
    v = tuple(int(x) for x in pin.split(".")) if pin else (0, 2)
    if v < (0, 7) and not opts.get("keep_cases"):
        for d, _, files in os.walk(os.path.join(root, "features")):
            for f in files:
                if not f.endswith(".test.md"):
                    continue
                feature = os.path.join(d, f[:-len(".test.md")] + ".md")
                if not os.path.exists(feature):
                    continue
                scenarios = {s.strip() for s in SCENARIO_RE.findall(open(feature).read())}
                p = os.path.join(d, f)
                text = open(p, newline="").read()
                if (cases := headings(text, scenarios)) != text:
                    open(p, "w", newline="").write(cases)
    git("rm", "-rq", "testdata/" + old)
    out = os.path.join(TD, new)
    shutil.copytree(dst, out, symlinks=True)


def main():
    work = tempfile.mkdtemp()
    for name in REMOVE:
        git("rm", "-rq", "testdata/" + name)
    for name in sorted(os.listdir(TD)):
        if name in REMOVE or name in LATER or not os.path.isdir(os.path.join(TD, name)):
            continue
        opts = OPTIONS.get(name, {})
        new = opts.get("name", re.sub(r"-v\d\d$", "", name))
        if name.endswith("-v10"):
            git("mv", "testdata/" + name, "testdata/" + new)
            continue
        convert(name, new, opts, work)
    shutil.rmtree(work)


main()
EOF
python3 "$d/convert.py" "$PWD" "$d/fdf"
rm -rf "$d"
~~~

Check: `ls testdata | wc -l` prints `166`, and no fixture's name ends in a
version: `ls testdata | grep -c -- '-v[0-9][0-9]$'` prints `0`.

git commit -m "test: convert the conformance fixtures to spec 1.0

Every fixture of a rule 1.0 keeps is migrated with fdf migrate, as a
bundle nobody has touched since would be, then tidied so that it fails
only for the rule it is about: the migration's log entry goes, the
vendored SPEC.md becomes the short stand-in the 1.0 fixtures keep, a
Context document migrate had to create is filled rather than left a stub
(one fixture drops SURFACES.md, the missing document it is about), and a
0.x test document's cases become the \`## <scenario name>\` headings 0.7
matches. Four hand edits remain: the ID of a feature that does not
exist, which migrate cannot know to be one, is written in full, and the
root file of bug-outside-bugs, which the closed root now reports on its
own, is gone. Each takes its name without its -vNN suffix, and its
expectations name the paths and IDs migrate gave it.

Removed: the fixtures of a document at the root, a feature, change,
practice, debt or log, or a Context document by another name, which the
closed root now reports whatever its type (F3 still checks the type of
SPEC.md, the one other document there); those of rules 1.0 no longer
has (0.2's paired directories and nested trail names, 0.5's one-line
declaration entries and a removal and addition of one name, 0.6's
Gherkin-only lexicon scan and its declared-names-only scan, an adopted
feature and a timestamp 0.6 did not check); and those another version
repeats: the 0.2 and 0.3 twins of the 0.4 fixtures, valid-bugs-v07
beside valid-bugs-v10, one missing Context document of three, and a
debt's R1 beside resource-missing-v10. The 1.0 fixtures lose their -v10
suffix; valid-v10 is valid-every-shape.

Three fixtures keep their pin for the validator's contract: a missing
root INDEX.md, a 0.1 pin and a pin newer than fdf.

The tests that copied a converted fixture follow it: fdf log, fdf mv
and fdf lexicon refuse valid-bugs pinned back to 0.7 (pinTo), the own
repository test validates valid-adopted at repo/docs/fdf, and the
submodule test mounts valid-done at docs/fdf.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
```

---

### Task 7: The validator checks spec 1.0 alone; the pin is required

fdf 1.x validates spec 1.x only (design §4, Decision 1). The validator
checks a bundle pinned to 1.0 (`supportedVersions`), and any other pin is
an F1 error that names its fix (`pinProblem`):

| Pin | F1 says |
|---|---|
| none | pins no `fdf_version`: write it in, as `fdf_version: "1.0"`, or migrate a bundle from before 1.0 |
| not a `MAJOR.MINOR` version (`1.0.0`, `v1.0`) | correct the pin, the newest supported version given as the example; not `fdf migrate` |
| older, a 0.x bundle | not a supported version: run `fdf migrate` |
| newer | newer than any version this fdf validates: upgrade fdf |

Validation stops there: the bundle is checked against no other rule, and
the report says it was not validated, with the versions this fdf checks. A
directory with no root `INDEX.md` is no bundle (`fdfroot.NoBundle`), where
0.7 validated it under 0.2's rules with a warning, which goes.

The 0.x code goes with it: the `visitV0` walk, the `stem`, `v5`, `v6` and
`v7` gates in the collection and the checks, 0.5's one-line declaration
entries and the excuse 0.6 gave any removal, `linkRe` and the 0.x branches
of `resolveLink` and `sectionLinks` (`sectionTargets` is the one reader of
a section's links), `dirName` and `registerDirs`, and the 0.6 lexicon scan
(`checkDomain` is now 0.7's, which 1.0 keeps). `walkV1` becomes `walk`,
and `documentV1` becomes `filed`.

The fixtures of the pin: `no-root-index` (`missing-root-index` renamed) is
no bundle; `pin-0x` (`wrong-fdf-version-pin` renamed) pins 0.7, and
`pin-newer-than-fdf` pins 1.1; `pin-missing` and `pin-not-a-version` are
new. Each holds a stray root file, and none reports it: validation stops at
F1. The CLI tests' minimal bundle pins the current version.

The root's type check (`root`) now sees only the vendored `SPEC.md` besides
the Context documents, since the closed root reports any other root file as
a stray, and no fixture held it once Task 6 removed the 0.x ones. Its error
now says that the spec is `type: Reference`, not to move the file to its
position. `vendored-spec-typed-bug` and `vendored-spec-typed-context` hold
it, which the merged `structural` map and the one list of Context documents
must keep. Before this task they catch the same error in its old words;
Step 3's rewording makes them pass.

**Files:**
- Modify: `cli/internal/bundle/validate.go`
- Modify: `cli/internal/bundle/walk.go`, `collect.go`, `changes.go`,
  `bugs.go`, `domain.go`, `lexicon.go`, `testcases.go`
- Modify: `cli/internal/bundle/pin_test.go`
- Modify: `cli/cmd/fdf/main_test.go` (`writeMinimalBundle`)
- Modify: `testdata/` (the five pin fixtures; `vendored-spec-typed-bug` and
  `vendored-spec-typed-context`, new)
- Modify: `cli/cmd/fdf/help.go` (the `validate` topic), `CLAUDE.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `specver.Parse`, `specver.Sort`, `fdfroot.NoBundle`.
- Produces: `pinProblem(pin string) string`, the F1 message for a pin this
  fdf does not validate, or ""; `newestSupported() string`. `walkV1`
  becomes `walk`, `documentV1` becomes `filed`, and `checkDomainV7`
  becomes `checkDomain`. Removed: `pinAtLeast`, `unsupportedPin`, the
  `visitV0` walk, `linkRe`, `sectionLinks`, `declLines`, `dirName`,
  `registerDirs`, `wordRe` and the 0.6 `checkDomain`; and the version
  arguments of `checkLogBody`, `parseDecls`, `strayDeclEntries`,
  `removals`, `featureHint`, `resolveLink`, `checkChangeIntegrity`,
  `checkReleaseChanges`, `checkBugIntegrity`, `readLexicon` and
  `scanBundle`.

```go
)

// pinAtLeast gates each rule by the spec version that introduced it. It
// compares versions by number, and a missing or unsupported pin is below
// every gate: such a bundle is validated under v0.2 rules.
func TestPinAtLeast(t *testing.T) {
	v := func(major, minor int) specver.Version { return specver.Version{Major: major, Minor: minor} }
	for _, tc := range []struct {
		pin  string
		gate specver.Version
		want bool
	}{
		{"0.7", v(0, 7), true},
		{"0.7", v(0, 5), true},
		{"0.6", v(0, 7), false},
		{"0.2", v(0, 2), true},
		{"0.7", v(1, 0), false},
		{"1.0", v(1, 0), true},
		{"1.0", v(0, 7), true}, // 1.0 keeps the rules 0.7 has
		{"", v(0, 2), false},
		{"0.9", v(0, 2), false}, // not a version this validator supports
		{"v0.7", v(0, 2), false},
		{"0.07", v(0, 2), false},
	} {
		if got := pinAtLeast(tc.pin, tc.gate); got != tc.want {
			t.Errorf("pinAtLeast(%q, %v) = %v; want %v", tc.pin, tc.gate, got, tc.want)
		}
	}
}

// version's bundles below every gate without any test noticing.

2. In `cli/internal/bundle/pin_test.go`, replace:

```go
// A pin this validator does not check names the fix: a newer fdf for a
// version past every supported one, such as the next minor, and `fdf migrate`
// for any other.
func TestUnsupportedPinNamesTheFix(t *testing.T) {
	var newest specver.Version
	for s := range supportedVersions {
		if v, _ := specver.Parse(s); newest.Less(v) {
			newest = v
		}
	}
	next := specver.Version{Major: newest.Major, Minor: newest.Minor + 1}.String()
	if msg := unsupportedPin(next); !strings.Contains(msg, "is newer than any version this fdf validates") || !strings.HasSuffix(msg, "upgrade fdf (F1)") {
		t.Errorf("unsupportedPin(%q) = %q; want it to ask for a newer fdf", next, msg)
	}
	for _, pin := range []string{"0.1", "v1.0", ""} {
		if msg := unsupportedPin(pin); !strings.HasSuffix(msg, "run `fdf migrate` (F1)") {
			t.Errorf("unsupportedPin(%q) = %q; want it to point at fdf migrate", pin, msg)
		}
	}
```

with:

```go
// A pin this validator does not check names the fix: a newer fdf for a
// version past every supported one, such as the next minor, `fdf migrate`
// for an older one, a correction for one that is not a MAJOR.MINOR
// version, and the pin itself when there is none. A supported pin is no
// problem.
func TestPinProblemNamesTheFix(t *testing.T) {
	var newest specver.Version
	for s := range supportedVersions {
		if v, _ := specver.Parse(s); newest.Less(v) {
			newest = v
		}
		if msg := pinProblem(s); msg != "" {
			t.Errorf("pinProblem(%q) = %q; want none", s, msg)
		}
	}
	for _, tc := range []struct{ pin, want string }{
		{specver.Version{Major: newest.Major, Minor: newest.Minor + 1}.String(), "is newer than any version this fdf validates (" + supportedList() + ") — upgrade fdf (F1)"},
		{specver.Version{Major: newest.Major + 1}.String(), "— upgrade fdf (F1)"},
		{"0.7", "is not a supported version (" + supportedList() + ") — run `fdf migrate` (F1)"},
		{"0.1", "— run `fdf migrate` (F1)"},
		{"1.0.0", `is not a MAJOR.MINOR version such as "` + newestSupported() + `" — correct the pin (F1)`},
		{"v1.0", "— correct the pin (F1)"},
		{"", "INDEX.md: pins no fdf_version"},
	} {
		if msg := pinProblem(tc.pin); !strings.Contains(msg, tc.want) {
			t.Errorf("pinProblem(%q) = %q; want it to say %q", tc.pin, msg, tc.want)
		}
	}
```

		"LOG.md":   "# Bundle Update Log\n\n## 2026-07-06\n* **Initialization**: created.\n",

var releaseStatuses = []string{"planned", "shipped"}

// supportedVersions are the spec versions this validator understands; a pin
// outside this set is an F1 error directing the user to `fdf migrate`, or to
// a newer fdf.
var supportedVersions = map[string]bool{"0.2": true, "0.3": true, "0.4": true, "0.5": true, "0.6": true, "0.7": true, "1.0": true}

// pinAtLeast reports whether a supported pin is spec version gate or later.
// An empty or unsupported pin validates under v0.2 rules (F1 reports the
// latter).
func pinAtLeast(pin string, gate specver.Version) bool {
	if !supportedVersions[pin] {
		return false
	}
	v, ok := specver.Parse(pin)
	return ok && v.AtLeast(gate)
}

6. In `cli/internal/bundle/validate.go`, replace:

```go
}

// unsupportedPin is F1's error for a pin this validator does not check. A
// version newer than every one it supports takes a newer fdf; any other,
// `fdf migrate`.
func unsupportedPin(pin string) string {
	newer, ok := specver.Parse(pin)
	for s := range supportedVersions {
		if v, _ := specver.Parse(s); !v.Less(newer) {
			ok = false
		}
	}
	if ok {
		return fmt.Sprintf("INDEX.md: fdf_version %q is newer than any version this fdf validates (%s) — upgrade fdf (F1)", pin, supportedList())
	}
	return fmt.Sprintf("INDEX.md: fdf_version %q is not a supported version (%s) — run `fdf migrate` (F1)", pin, supportedList())
}
```

with:

```go
}

// pinProblem is F1's error for a root INDEX.md whose pin this validator
// cannot check a bundle against, naming the fix, or "" for a supported pin:
// a missing pin is written in, one that is not a MAJOR.MINOR version is
// corrected, a version newer than every one this fdf validates takes a newer
// fdf, and an older one, a 0.x bundle, `fdf migrate`.
func pinProblem(pin string) string {
	if supportedVersions[pin] {
		return ""
	}
	v, ok := specver.Parse(pin)
	switch {
	case pin == "":
		return fmt.Sprintf("INDEX.md: pins no fdf_version — the version of the spec a bundle follows is pinned in its frontmatter, as fdf_version: \"%s\"; a bundle from before 1.0 is upgraded with `fdf migrate` (F1)", newestSupported())
	case !ok:
		return fmt.Sprintf("INDEX.md: fdf_version %q is not a MAJOR.MINOR version such as %q — correct the pin (F1)", pin, newestSupported())
	}
	for s := range supportedVersions {
		if w, _ := specver.Parse(s); !w.Less(v) {
			return fmt.Sprintf("INDEX.md: fdf_version %q is not a supported version (%s) — run `fdf migrate` (F1)", pin, supportedList())
		}
	}
	return fmt.Sprintf("INDEX.md: fdf_version %q is newer than any version this fdf validates (%s) — upgrade fdf (F1)", pin, supportedList())
}

// newestSupported is the newest version this validator checks.
func newestSupported() string {
	vs := strings.Split(supportedList(), ", ")
	return vs[len(vs)-1]
}
```
